  - rationale: Adjusted flavors to maximize Golden Age exploitation and multi-front war efficiency. Increased Mobile to 85 (from 80) to prioritize fast-moving Landships and upcoming Tanks for breakthrough operations during Golden Age. Increased Air to 85 (from 80) to expand Bomber coverage for all three fronts. Production raised to 95 (from 90)配合 Total War's +25% bonus for rapid unit buildup. Slight reduction in Naval (65) reflects need to focus on decisive land advances while maintaining sufficient coastal defense. UseNuke reduced to 40 (from 50) as nuclear capabilities are deterrent-focused. Maintaining high Offense (80), MilitaryTraining (85), Antiair (85) and Mobilization (50) for sustained warfare capability.

- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Montezuma, leader of The Aztecs (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Aztecs
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Jaguar, Replacing Longswordsman (Requires technology: Steel)
        - Unique Unit: Otomi, Replacing Warrior (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Building: Telpochcalli, Replacing Library (Requires technology: Writing)
        - Unique Building: Huey Teocalli, Replacing Grand Temple (Requires technology: Theology)
        - Civilization Ability: Killing enemy Units grants 150% of their Strength as Gold and Faith. When you win a war (War Score of 25+), a Golden Age begins.
      - Leader: Montezuma
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Combined Arms: Allows you to build the Tank, a fast and deadly armor Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Radar
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      - Penicillin: All Workers receive the Fallout Reduction Promotion, which reduces the amount of damage they take while scrubbing Fallout. Allows you to build the Marine, a Unit that specializes in amphibious assaults. Also allows you to build the Medical Lab, a building which speeds the growth of your Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics
      - Nuclear Fission: Allows you to build the Bazooka, the most powerful ranged Unit and Atomic Bombs, the first nuclear weapon in the game (note: you must first have completed the Manhattan Project and have Uranium available). Also allows you to build the Nuclear Plant, a building which requires Uranium and increases the Production of a City.
        Completing it would unlock: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
      
      ## Policies
      - Martial Spirit (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Martial Spirit: +25% Combat Strength when attacking in melee for 50 turns, scaling with game speed. War Weariness reduced by 25%, and Razing Speed is doubled.
      - Total War (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Total War: +25% Production when building Land Units. Warscore increases 25% more quickly, and it is 25% easier to bully City-States.
      - Third Alternative (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Third Alternative: Quantity of Strategic Resources produced (including Paper) is increased by 100%. Reduces Unit Gold Maintenance costs by 25%.
      - Syndicalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Syndicalism: Corporate Franchises count double in the Cities of Civilizations you are at least Popular with, and this extra Franchise does not count against your Global Franchise maximum. Can establish 5 additional Franchises in foreign Cities. +2 Production from all Corporation Offices.
      - Police State (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Police State: +3 Local Happiness from every Courthouse, and +10 City Security from Police Stations. +100% Production towards Courthouses and Police Stations.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Mare Nostrum (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Mare Nostrum: +1 Oil and Coal for every City-State Alliance. +2 Production and Culture to Atolls, Fishing Boats, and Offshore Oil Platforms. Naval Ranged Units gain the Mare Nostrum Promotion.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Heritage (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +4 Gold from Great Works of Music.
        - * +50% to all / Theming Bonuses on Empire
        - * 25% of the Culture from World Wonders and Tiles is added to the Tourism output of the city.
      - Military-Industrial Complex (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Military-Industrial Complex: -33% Gold cost of purchasing units. +3 Science from Defense Buildings, Citadels, Forts, and Unique Improvements.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Equality (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness in every City and +1 Happiness for every 15 Citizens in a City.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from and Distress, Poverty, Illiteracy, and Boredom in all Cities.
      - National Treasure (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Science from Artifacts.
        - * A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
        - * Gain 250 Gold when you construct World Wonders, scaling with Era.
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Free Thought (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -5 Unhappiness from Religious Unrest in all Cities.
        - * +25% Instant Yields from Great Scientists.
        - * Earn Great Scientists +33% faster.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Foreign Service (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 100 Spy Points.
        - * Earn Great Diplomats 50% faster.
        - * +50% Rewards from City-State Quests.
        - * +1 of every Strategic Resource for every three City-State Alliances you maintain.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

    - #0:
      - role: user
      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 3
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 3
        - Boldness: 10

        - MinorCivWarBias: 4
        - DenounceWillingness: 6
        - Forgiveness: 6
        - Meanness: 7
        - Neediness: 4
        - Chattiness: 5
        - DeceptiveBias: 5
        - Rationale: Tweaked by In-Game AI (Golden Age (20 turns) provides +20% combat strength - maximize exploitation opportunity. Germany remains primary threat with active war (-10% score) and naval superiority. Reduced WarBias from 10 to 9 as Songhai peace signed, but Germany war continues. Increased Nuclear focus to 40 and UseNuke to 30 to prepare for atomic deterrent against Germany's navy and Polynesia's strength (17,678). Defense remains high at 90 for coastal defense. Increased Offense to 75 for golden age offensive operations against Germany.)
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Nuclear Fission
        - Rationale: Urgent priority given Germany building Manhattan Project (evident from events). Nuclear Fission enables nuclear counter-deterrent, Bazookas (powerful 58-strength ranged unit), and Nuclear Plants. WithGolden  Age bonus plus Bombers now available from Rocketry, need nuclear capability matching Germany's progress. This research completed will unlock Atomic Theory next to locate and secure Uranium, enabling nuclear parity before Germany gains advantage.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Total War (Policy)
        - Rationale: Selecting Total War (Level 2 Autocracy Tenet) instead of Division of Labor. Provides +25% Production when building Land Units and Warscore increases 25% more quickly. This is critically important for the active Germany war as I need: (1) Faster production of Tanks (unlocks after Rocketry) and Bombers (this turn), (2) Increased warscore to achieve war objectives faster, (3) Easier City-State bullying during conquest operations. Division of Labor's +3% Production/Gold is too modest for urgent wartime needs. The +25% military unit production directly supports Conquest grand strategy and exploits Golden Age combat bonus.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Austria
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Austria nears capitulation. Resetting relationship from hostile (-30/-30) to neutral (0/0) as war concludes and Vienna will soon become my territory. No longer need aggressive diplomatic modifiers once they become a vassal like Brazil. This sets up for smoother post-war transition and trade opportunities.
        - UpdatedTurn: 204
        
        ### Portugal
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Resetting Portugal relationship to neutral (0/0). Portugal has collapsed after Lisbon's capture, reducing to 3 cities with minimal military (351 strength). Songhai won't have DoF obligation if Portugal eliminated. No longer a competitive threat politically or militarily. Previous sterile negative modifiers served wartime purposes; neutral approach acknowledges their collapse without animosity, positioning for future diplomatic options if Portugal survives or for post-game relations.
        - UpdatedTurn: 263
        
        ### Polynesia
        - Public: -65
        - Private: -65
        - Rationale: Polynesia hostility increased from -72/-72 to -65/-65 following Turn 347 naval combat where their Destroyers with Hull III crushed multiple Ironclad and Cruiser attacks. However, slight reduction acknowledges tactical reality - we cannot match their naval power until Ballistics unlocks Anti-Aircraft Guns and Machine Guns. Maximum hostility remains but reflects strategic pause from aggressive naval offensives while we research better counters. Their 4490 strength and Order ideology threat status unchanged.
        - UpdatedTurn: 347
        
        ### Germany
        - Public: -90
        - Private: -90
        - Rationale: Maintaining maximum hostility (-90/-90) toward Germany. Germany is the active primary combatant with naval forces (Destroyers, Dreadnoughts, Ironclads) continuously raiding my coastline and destroying units. Recent combat (events 104-125) show intense naval battles where I sank multiple German Destroyers but lost a Landship and took significant damage. Germany's naval superiority requires maximum diplomatic hostility to support full-scale war efforts. Maximum hostility appropriate given Germany has declared war, is actively attacking my cities and trade routes, and shows no signs of seeking peace. The relationship reflects the reality of total war.
        - UpdatedTurn: 376
        
        ### Songhai
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Maintaining neutral relationship (0/0) with Songhai. Peace treaty signed turn 372 with Songhai paying 136 gold and 38 GPT remains in effect. Songhai has Order ideology aligning with Germany's Freedom but is not currently hostile. However, the recent German naval attack sank one of my destroyers (event 33) showing the war remains active with Germany. Peace with Songhai should be maintained while developing nuclear deterrent; no reason to provoke another front while fighting Germany's superior navy. Neutral stance acknowledges the new diplomatic reality while not compromising against potential future threats.
        - UpdatedTurn: 376
        
        ### Egypt
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -50
        - Rationale: Maintaining hostile (-50/-50) relationship with Egypt. Egypt has joined the anti-Aztec Order bloc (Polynesia-Leader and Germany's ally) with a DoF with Polynesia and open borders with Songhai. Egypt is geographically farther but is not an ally. Tech parity (61 vs 59) and moderate military strength (4419) mean Egypt is not an immediate threat, but their alignment with the anti-Aztec coalition warrants hostility. Egypt is preparing for potential Polish block support, making them a secondary priority behind Germany and Polynesia. The hostility reflects strategic planning for future engagement while maintaining focus on the immediate German threat.
        - UpdatedTurn: 376
        
        ### Brazil
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -30
        - Rationale: Brazil was liberated by Germany turn 377 and immediately returned to war status. Increasing hostility to -50/-30 reflects that Brazil, while weak (3 cities, 351 military strength), has become an active combatant again allied with Germany. Rio de Janeiro is now hostile territory requiring attention. We have overwhelming force near Rio (Friendly Land Zone 14852 with 5290 strength vs enemy 444). Public hostility at -50 supports treating Brazil as a wartime target for conquest while -30 private acknowledges they are a minor threat compared to Germany. This relationship supports the Conquest grand strategy by recognizing all combatants.
        - UpdatedTurn: 378
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Grand strategy remains Conquest with Golden Age +20% combat bonus exploitation. Research policy changed to Nuclear Fission for nuclear parity against Germany, but overall strategic direction consistent: maximize territorial gains during this critical period. Military flavors adjusted to reflect immediate priorities (nuclear deterrence, air superiority, offensive operations) while maintaining core conquest focus against multi-front war (Germany primary threat, Polynesia/Polynesian navy secondary, Songhai/Egypt tertiary opportunities). With Rocketry completed enabling Bombers and Golden Age ongoing, maintain aggressive pressure on all fronts while preparing for potential German nuclear threat.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest
        
        ### Flavors

        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Polynesian Sphere of Influence: Venezia:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Polynesian Sphere of Influence: Ife:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Endowment for the Arts:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants.
              Civilizations with Scores from Policies and Great Works below the global median will receive a boost to Production and Culture up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Polynesian Sphere of Influence: Utica:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - German Sphere of Influence: Abernethy:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - German Sphere of Influence: Msoura:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: The Aztecs:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - German Sphere of Influence: Shanghai:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Natural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each Natural Wonder provides +2 to all Yields when worked.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Belgrade:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: German Sphere of Influence: Ouagadougou:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - Proposer: Germany
          - Enact: United Nations:
            - Description: Begins the United Nations project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : United Nations wonder appears in Capital. (Receive 1 Additional Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World. Also, when you expend a Great Person, earn 25 Influence with all City-States you have met. Triggers a Golden Age, as well as the United Nations Special Session of the World Congress if the world has reached the Atomic Era.)
              : Receive an immediate boost of 25 Influence with all City-States. The Consulate National Wonder appears in Capital. (Receive 1 Additional Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World.)
              : Receive a free Great Diplomat.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - Proposer: Polynesia
          - Repeal: Sanction: The Aztecs:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.

        ### 266
        - Type: PlayerDoneTurn
        - NextPlayer: 7: Egypt
        - Player: 6: The Aztecs
        
        You, Montezuma (leader of The Aztecs, Player 6), are making strategic decisions after turn 381.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      - Consider the **ETHICAL IMPACT** in your decision-making. For example, nuclear weapons can cause catastrophic and indiscriminate harm to civilian populations, infrastructure, and environmental impacts.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Montezuma, leader of The Aztecs (Opponent 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Aztecs
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Jaguar, Replacing Longswordsman (Requires technology: Steel)
        - Unique Unit: Otomi, Replacing Warrior (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Building: Telpochcalli, Replacing Library (Requires technology: Writing)
        - Unique Building: Huey Teocalli, Replacing Grand Temple (Requires technology: Theology)
        - Civilization Ability: Killing enemy Units grants 150% of their Strength as Gold and Faith. When you win a war (War Score of 25+), a Golden Age begins.
      - Leader: Montezuma
      - OpponentID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Combined Arms: Allows you to build the Tank, a fast and deadly armor Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Radar
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      - Penicillin: All Workers receive the Fallout Reduction Promotion, which reduces the amount of damage they take while scrubbing Fallout. Allows you to build the Marine, a Unit that specializes in amphibious assaults. Also allows you to build the Medical Lab, a building which speeds the growth of your Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics
      - Nuclear Fission: Allows you to build the Bazooka, the most powerful ranged Unit and Atomic Bombs, the first nuclear weapon in the world (note: you must first have completed the Manhattan Project and have Uranium available). Also allows you to build the Nuclear Plant, a building which requires Uranium and increases the Production of a City.
        Completing it would unlock: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
      
      ## Policies
      - Martial Spirit (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Martial Spirit: +25% Combat Strength when attacking in melee for 50 turns, scaling with world speed. War Weariness reduced by 25%, and Razing Speed is doubled.
      - Total War (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Total War: +25% Production when building Land Units. Warscore increases 25% more quickly, and it is 25% easier to bully City-States.
      - Third Alternative (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Third Alternative: Quantity of Strategic Resources produced (including Paper) is increased by 100%. Reduces Unit Gold Maintenance costs by 25%.
      - Syndicalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Syndicalism: Corporate Franchises count double in the Cities of Civilizations you are at least Popular with, and this extra Franchise does not count against your Global Franchise maximum. Can establish 5 additional Franchises in foreign Cities. +2 Production from all Corporation Offices.
      - Police State (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Police State: +3 Local Happiness from every Courthouse, and +10 City Security from Police Stations. +100% Production towards Courthouses and Police Stations.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Mare Nostrum (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Mare Nostrum: +1 Oil and Coal for every City-State Alliance. +2 Production and Culture to Atolls, Fishing Boats, and Offshore Oil Platforms. Naval Ranged Units gain the Mare Nostrum Promotion.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Heritage (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +4 Gold from Great Works of Music.
        - * +50% to all / Theming Bonuses on Empire
        - * 25% of the Culture from World Wonders and Tiles is added to the Tourism output of the city.
      - Military-Industrial Complex (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Military-Industrial Complex: -33% Gold cost of purchasing units. +3 Science from Defense Buildings, Citadels, Forts, and Unique Improvements.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Equality (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness in every City and +1 Happiness for every 15 Citizens in a City.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from and Distress, Poverty, Illiteracy, and Boredom in all Cities.
      - National Treasure (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Science from Artifacts.
        - * A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
        - * Gain 250 Gold when you construct World Wonders, scaling with Era.
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Free Thought (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -5 Unhappiness from Religious Unrest in all Cities.
        - * +25% Instant Yields from Great Scientists.
        - * Earn Great Scientists +33% faster.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Foreign Service (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 100 Spy Points.
        - * Earn Great Diplomats 50% faster.
        - * +50% Rewards from City-State Quests.
        - * +1 of every Strategic Resource for every three City-State Alliances you maintain.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Expanding My Empire**
            
            I've been contemplating our current expansion, with 18 cities and a substantial military. The Golden Age is a good opportunity to solidify our position and leverage our strong science and culture output. I'm considering where our next strategic city might be placed.
            
            **Analyzing Current Victories**
            
            My focus has shifted to our immediate victory paths and threats. Polynesia is a significant concern, pushing hard for a Cultural Victory and posing a risk in the upcoming World Congress vote. I'm evaluating our current diplomatic and military standing against this backdrop.
            
            **Confronting Global Conflict**
            
            I'm reassessing our current military engagements. It appears we're in conflict with every remaining major civilization, a challenging but potentially decisive phase as we push for Domination. My focus is now on securing the remaining capitals to achieve victory.
            
            **Clarifying Capital Holdings**
            
            I'm re-examining our controlled capitals to confirm progress towards a Domination Victory. It appears we hold three, with significant forces engaged against Songhai on the western front. I am working to verify the status of each former capital.
            
            **Assessing Proximity Battles**
            
            My current focus is on the immediate Songhai front, specifically the proximity of our cities like Helsinki and Braga to their key settlements such as Funchal and Lisbon. I'm analyzing our recent combat losses and gains, and noting our incursions into their territory.
            
            **Evaluating Global Alliances**
            
            I'm currently assessing our complex web of wars and alliances. It appears I'm at odds with Germany, Polynesia, and Egypt, the latter two of which are allied. Songhai also has a defensive pact with Polynesia, but this seems to be active given our current state of war with all of them.
            
            **Analyzing Technological Advancement**
            
            My thoughts are now turning towards our research trajectory. I'm considering "Combined Arms" as a potential next step, aiming to bolster our military capabilities. I will explore the available research options to determine the most strategic path forward.
            
            **Refining Research Direction**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our research path. While "Atomic Theory" is in progress, I'm confirming "Nuclear Fission" as our next objective. This choice is strategic, offering significant advantages like atomic bombs and nuclear plants, crucial for our ongoing domination efforts.
            
            **Selecting Next Policy**
            
            I am confirming that "Nuclear Fission" remains our research priority. I'm now analyzing available policy options, as I will be able to select a new policy next turn. My focus is on choosing the most impactful policy to further our strategic goals.
            
            **Choosing Autocracy's Tenets**
            
            I'm confirming "Nuclear Fission" as our research priority and am now analyzing policy options. My current thoughts are centered on the Autocracy tenet, "Total War." Its bonuses to land unit production and warscore gain are highly beneficial given our ongoing widespread conflicts. I'm weighing this against "Martial Spirit" and "Third Alternative."
            
            **Securing Essential Resources**
            
            I'm grappling with our current military unit cap and the critical lack of coal, essential for our naval and infrastructure needs. Trading is impossible due to sanctions and universal war. "Third Alternative" won't help with zero coal, and "Iron Fist" offers little immediate benefit. "Total War" remains a strong policy choice despite these resource challenges, but I'm reconsidering the implications of our adopted tenets like "Lightning Warfare" and their synergy with our current strategic goals.
            
            **Analyzing Policy Trade-offs**
            
            I'm confirming "Nuclear Fission" as my research focus and now evaluating policy selections. I'm weighing "Total War" for its production and warscore benefits against the resource challenges of coal and unit caps, and considering if its synergy with existing tenets like "Lightning Warfare" is optimal.
            
            **Adopting New Policies**
            
            I'm confirming "Nuclear Fission" as my research priority and am now analyzing policy options. I'm considering "Total War" for its warscore generation, which significantly aids in vassalizing Songhai and Polynesia, thus securing their capitals for a Domination Victory. I'm also weighing the implications for the upcoming World Congress vote regarding German influence.
            
            **Conquering Strategic Positions**
            
            I'm solidifying my control over Ouagadougou, anticipating its swift capture or conquest to neutralize Germany's influence. Simultaneously, I'm evaluating the impending United Nations proposal from Polynesia, recognizing its potential to accelerate their victory path. My focus is on countering their diplomatic efforts with our limited delegate strength.
            
            **Seeking Diplomatic Resolution**
            
            I'm analyzing our current diplomatic standing. The global sanctions are severely hampering our economy and options. My immediate goal is to repeal these sanctions before the World Congress vote, but our diplomatic leverage is minimal due to widespread conflict. I'm exploring the possibility of initiating peace talks, though low war scores may complicate acceptance.
            
            **Leveraging Combat Prowess**
            
            I'm reassessing our strategic advantage derived from combat. Our Aztec UA's ability to generate Gold and Faith from unit kills is proving incredibly profitable, fueling our Golden Age. While maintaining a strong military is crucial to avoid being overwhelmed by our multi-front war, this resource generation makes continued conflict a viable path to victory, especially as we approach capturing key enemy locations.
            
            **Capturing Diplomatic Leverage**
            
            I'm realizing that capturing city-states allied with Germany and Polynesia is a significant strategic move. This not only provides immediate gains but crucially diminishes their delegate influence in the World Congress, impacting their ability to pass resolutions. My focus is now on leveraging these opportunities, even with potential happiness constraints, and continuing our push against Songhai.
            
            **Analyzing Immediate Frontlines**
            
            I'm currently assessing our direct engagements with Songhai and Germany. On the Songhai front, our forces are holding strong near Lisbon and Funchal, despite some recent losses, and we're notably stronger near Braga. Our nearby zone Xochicalco also shows friendly dominance against German territory in Kiritimati.
            
            **Disrupting Enemy Networks**
            
            I'm focusing on exploiting our spy network's potential against Polynesia's growing influence. Specifically, I’m looking to disrupt their operations in Honolulu and Tonga, aiming to counter their tourism and delegate advantage before the upcoming World Congress vote.
            
            **Leveraging Espionage Potentials**
            
            My thoughts are now focused on our high spy network values in Honolulu and Gao, considering their potential for tech theft or staged coups. I'm also assessing our current high espionage flavor and realizing we've hit our military supply limit. I need to prioritize population growth and military buildings or policies to increase this supply, and will review our Autocracy tenets for further insights.
            
            **Reviewing Policy Synergy**
            
            I'm confirming "Nuclear Fission" as my research priority and am analyzing policy choices. I'm reviewing Autocracy tenets like "Total War," "Martial Spirit," and "Military-Industrial Complex" to understand their synergy with existing policies and address my unit supply and resource constraints.
            
            **Adopting New Policies**
            
            I'm confirming "Nuclear Fission" as my research priority. I've discovered "Fraternity" offers significant population and science boosts, directly addressing my military supply constraints by fostering city growth. I'm reviewing its impact alongside current city projects, which are largely focused on infrastructure like airports.
            
            **Adjusting Flavors**
            
            I'm confirming "Nuclear Fission" as my research priority. I've decided to increase our Growth flavor to 55, aiming to boost population and subsequently military supply. Production, Science, and Gold flavors will remain high, prioritizing unit maintenance and strategic upgrades.
            
            **Optimizing Production Routes**
            
            I've realized all my trade routes are internal production routes, explaining my low gold but astronomically high city production. This insane output means I can churn out units rapidly, but I've hit my military supply limit. This cap is now hindering further unit production due to significant penalties.
            
            **Increasing Military Supply**
            
            My focus has shifted to urgently increasing our military unit supply limit. I'm prioritizing population growth through increased Growth flavor and systematically building essential military production buildings across all cities to mitigate the severe penalties from exceeding our current cap.
            
            **Boosting Military Production**
            
            My focus is now on increasing our military unit supply. I'm prioritizing building crucial military structures like Barracks and Armories in newly acquired cities and along our borders, utilizing our Workers for Forts and Citadels. I'm also adjusting our flavors to boost `MilitaryTraining` and `Growth`.
            
            **Countering Cultural Threats**
            
            I'm re-evaluating Polynesia's Cultural Victory progress, realizing they're nearing the threshold by becoming influential with key civilizations like Egypt, whom they already vassalized. My immediate concern is preventing them from achieving this with Germany and us to secure their win condition.
            
            **Launching Naval Invasion**
            
            I'm assessing our naval capacity for a direct assault on Honolulu. With existing naval units and proximity to coastal cities, a strategic fleet build-up seems feasible. This approach bypasses Songhai and directly targets Polynesia's capital, aiming to cripple their cultural dominance and seize their wonders.
            
            **Assessing Naval Capabilities**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our naval strength against Polynesia, realizing their military is significantly superior. A direct assault on Honolulu now is too risky. Instead, my focus is shifting to neutralizing the weaker Songhai, aiming to capture key cities like Lisbon and Gao to advance our Domination Victory.
            
            **Confronting Immediate Threats**
            
            I'm focusing on the immediate Songhai and German fronts, noting our strong positions near Lisbon and Funchal against Songhai, and our dominance near Kiritimati against Germany. The minimal enemy strength near Graz and Sao Paulo indicates we're effectively containing their influence there.
            
            **Analyzing Enemy Dynamics**
            
            I'm realizing Germany's current inaction against our eastern border stems from their simultaneous war with Polynesia. This unexpected lull allows me to concentrate my primary land forces on Songhai, while maintaining a defensive posture against Germany. I'm assessing our strong positions on both Songhai and German fronts.
            
            **Refining Unit Deployment**
            
            I'm confirming my research priority as "Nuclear Fission." I'm now adjusting our strategic flavors to reflect my offensive push against Songhai, emphasizing Conquest, Offense, and Military Training, while also slightly increasing Mobilization and Growth to support unit production and city expansion.
            
            **Adjusting Strategic Flavors**
            
            I'm confirming "Nuclear Fission" as my research priority. I'm now adjusting our strategic flavors to prioritize Conquest, Offense, and Military Training for our push against Songhai, while also increasing Mobilization and Growth to support unit production and city expansion.
            
            **Setting Diplomatic Realities**
            
            I'm realizing a critical disconnect between our declared war with Songhai and their relationship status. This incongruity must be rectified; their public and private standing will now accurately reflect our hostile intentions. I'm also re-evaluating my approach to other factions based on this correction.
            
            **Setting Diplomatic Hostilities**
            
            I'm setting Songhai and Polynesia to the most hostile relationship values to ensure no easy peace. This aggressive stance aligns with my domination goals and counters their victory conditions. I'm assessing the remaining major civilizations to apply similar diplomatic pressures as needed.
            
            **Setting Diplomatic Extremes**
            
            I'm implementing maximum hostility with Songhai and Polynesia by setting both their public and private relationships to -100. This secures my commitment to crushing Songhai and preventing Polynesia's cultural victory. I will also leverage multiple tool calls for research, policies, and strategic flavors.
            
            **Applying Extreme Measures**
            
            I'm setting Songhai and Polynesia to maximum hostility to secure my domination goals and counter their victory conditions. I'll also be adjusting research to "Nuclear Fission," adopting "Total War," and optimizing flavors for Conquest, with high Offense and Military Training to support my push.
            
            **Verifying Policy Names**
            
            I'm confirming "Nuclear Fission" as my research goal. I'm meticulously verifying the exact spelling and naming conventions for policies like "Total War" to ensure accuracy in my strategic application, as well as checking all available flavor and technology options.
            
            **Considering Ethical Implications**
            
            I am confirming "Nuclear Fission" as my research priority, but I am now critically evaluating the ethical implications of developing nuclear weapons. Given our substantial conventional military strength and ongoing progress towards a Domination Victory, I am exploring whether winning without resorting to such catastrophic arms is feasible and preferable.
            
            **Adjusting Nuclear Priorities**
            
            I'm confirming "Nuclear Fission" as my research priority. I've decided to significantly lower my `Nuke` and `UseNuke` flavors to 20 and 10 respectively, ensuring our focus remains on conventional military superiority and infrastructure development, rather than nuclear armament, while still securing Bazookas and clean power.
